# Ownership & Insider Behavior — UBER

**No proxy statement (DEF 14A) is present in the data pool.** `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` confirms Item 12 ("Security Ownership of Certain Beneficial Owners and Management") of the FY25 10-K is "incorporated herein by reference to the 2026 Proxy Statement," which is absent from this pool [FY25 10-K, p.33158-33163]. In place of the proxy's beneficial-ownership table, this agent uses the Capital IQ ownership/insider-transaction export (`UberTechnologiesIncNYSEUBER_CIQReportLandscape`), which carries its own Top-25-Holders (institutions, corporations, individuals/insiders, funds), Public Ownership Summary, and 3/6-month insider-transaction tables sourced from Section 16 filings and 13D/13G-equivalent aggregation — a data-vendor source (§4 tier 5), cited as such throughout, not presented as the filing's own beneficial-ownership table. Per the partial-data rule, this caps Shareholder-friendliness contribution but does not block the read: the vendor export is a real, dated, sourced dataset, not a web-sourced guess. Ownership (a static snapshot) and insider transactions (the behavior signal) are reported separately below, as required.

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| CEO — Dara Khosrowshahi | 0.060% of CSO (1,225,802 sh; $87.8mm mkt value) | Common (single class) | Granted — nearly all identifiable share activity in the pool is $0-value RSU vesting, not open-market cash purchase (see §2) | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders), data as of extraction ~2026-08-06] |
| Other insiders (aggregate — officers & directors, ex-CEO) | 0.116% of CSO (2,360,305 sh = 3,586,107 total − 1,225,802 CEO; $169.0mm mkt value) | Common (single class) | Granted — RSU/PSU vesting and director stock-unit conversions; no open-market cash purchase identified in the pool for any named officer or director | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders), data as of extraction ~2026-08-06] |
| **Total Individuals/Insiders** | **0.18% of total shares outstanding** (3,586,107 sh of 2,042,560,121 sh out; $244.5–256.8mm mkt value, two CIQ tables differ slightly on pricing date) | Common (single class) | Granted (equity compensation) — see §2 | [CIQ Report Landscape, Public Ownership Summary + Top 25 Holders (Individuals/Insiders) Total row] |
| Largest institutional holders | BlackRock 7.417% ($10,812.2mm); Capital Research and Management 6.670% ($9,723.2mm); Vanguard Capital Management 6.569% ($9,575.2mm); State Street Investment Mgmt 4.453% ($6,490.7mm); Public Investment Fund (Saudi Arabia sovereign wealth fund) 3.578% ($5,216.1mm) | Common (single class) | N/A (third-party institutional holders) | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders / Top 25 Holders (Institutions)] |
| Promoter / controlling bloc | **None.** Uber has no founder, promoter, or family controlling block. Institutions hold 84.00% of CSO in aggregate; "Public and Other" (retail/unclassified) holds 15.34%; Corporations (public+private) 0.03%; State Owned Shares (unitemized in the pool) 0.12%; Individuals/Insiders 0.18% | Common (single class) | N/A | [CIQ Report Landscape, Public Ownership Summary]; [FY25 10-K, p.5289 — 1,207 holders of record as of Feb-10-2026]; [Public Company Profile — 99.7% float] |

**Total insider ownership: ~0.18% of shares outstanding.** This is extremely low in percentage terms for a company of Uber's scale — a direct consequence of Dara Khosrowshahi being hired from Expedia in September 2017 to replace ousted co-founder Travis Kalanick, not a founder or long-tenured accumulator of equity [`management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md`, §"Founder / owner-operator status"]. In dollar terms, the CEO's stake ($87.8mm) is meaningful to him personally, but it represents a rounding error against Uber's $139.3bn market capitalization [Public Company Profile, Last price $70.74, Aug-06-2026] and confers no economic control. Co-founder Garrett Camp holds a non-voting Board Observer role only, with no disclosed equity position in this pool and no executive function [`01_management-and-track-record.md`].

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

The CIQ export's individually-itemized transaction table covers the **last 6 months** (Feb-2026 through Jul-2026); the pool does not carry a full 12-month itemized Section-16 history. The aggregate summary statistics do cover 3-month and 6-month windows for **all** insiders (not just the Top 25 named individuals), and are reported here in full; where the 12-month window is not covered, that gap is stated rather than extrapolated.

**Aggregate summary (all insiders, per CIQ):**

| Window | # Purchases | Shares Purchased | # Sales | Shares Sold | Net (shares) | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Last 3 months | 16 | 51,482 | 15 | 55,091 | (3,609) net sold | [CIQ Report Landscape, Insider Trading Activity Summary] |
| Last 6 months | 44 | 574,497 | 39 | 608,470 | (33,973) net sold | [CIQ Report Landscape, Insider Trading Activity Summary] |

Both windows show near-balanced purchase/sale share counts — net selling of well under 0.002% of the 2,042.6mm shares outstanding either way. This magnitude is not a discretionary-conviction signal; it is consistent with the mechanical share-count offset of RSU vesting followed by sell-to-cover tax withholding described below.

**Named individual transactions (last 6 months, Top 25 Individuals/Insiders):**

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes (10b5-1?) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Khosrowshahi, Dara (CEO) | Buy | 243,258 sh, $0 value | Mar-16-2026 | RSU vesting event (zero transaction value = no cash paid) | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Purchases] |
| Khosrowshahi, Dara (CEO) | **Sell** | (253,700) sh, **$18,941,242** at $74.66 | Mar-16-2026 | Same-day sell-to-cover following the vest above — the single largest-dollar transaction of any insider in the window. No explicit Rule 10b5-1 plan is named for Khosrowshahi in the pool (unlike Macdonald's, below); the recurring monthly pattern and the company's policy requiring covered persons to trade via 10b5-1 plans make a scheduled/automatic sale the more likely read — **inference, not from filings**, on plan status specifically | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Sales]; [FY25 10-K, Insider Trading Policy §H/VII (10b5-1 plan requirement for board/officers/ELT)] |
| Krishnamurthy C.F.A., Balaji (CFO, appointed effective Feb-16-2026) | Buy | 22,453 sh, **$1,599,780** at $71.25–71.32 | Feb-24-2026 | Nonzero transaction value (unlike the $0 RSU-vest entries) — consistent with an option exercise or new-hire equity conversion tied to his CFO appointment, not a discretionary open-market cash purchase; ambiguous in the pool, flagged rather than asserted | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Purchases] |
| West J.D., Derek A. (Chief Legal Officer); Ceremony, Glen (Chief Accounting Officer); Krishnamurthy C.F.A., Balaji (CFO); Hazelbaker, Jill (Pres., Corp. Affairs); MacDonald, Andrew G. (President & COO) | Buy ($0) + Sell (nonzero $) in near-identical share counts | Recurring monthly, e.g. ~3,600–4,700 sh per name | 16th of every month, Feb–Jul 2026 | Textbook scheduled RSU-vest-then-sell-to-cover pattern, repeating every month for the same five names in near-identical amounts — mechanical tax-withholding administration, not discretionary trading | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Purchases + Recent Insider Sales tables] |
| Sugar, Thain, Wynaendts, Alnowaiser, Trujillo (independent directors) | Small buys ($0 value, ~338–5,415 sh) around May-03/May-04-2026; Wynaendts and Alnowaiser also show small sells (~$42–57K) same date | Apr–May 2026 | Routine director equity-comp unit conversions, not open-market purchases | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Purchases + Sales] |
| MacDonald, Andrew G. (President & COO) | Terminated a pre-arranged Rule 10b5-1 trading plan (adopted Sep-8-2025) that had provided for exercise/sale of up to 125,000 shares between Dec-24-2025 and Jan-27-2026 | Plan terminated Dec-18-2025 | Explicit 10b5-1 disclosure (Item 9B) — the only NEO with a named, dated 10b5-1 plan in this pool | [FY25 10-K, Item 9B "Rule 10b5-1 Trading Plans"] |

**Net insider activity: net selling in dollar terms, driven almost entirely by one large sale.** Excluding the CEO's $18.9mm March sale, the remaining transactions net out to modest, offsetting monthly vest-and-withhold flows. **No genuine open-market cash purchase by the CEO or any named executive officer was found in the pool's 6-month window** — every "purchase" with a $0 transaction value is an RSU vesting credit, not a cash buy; the one nonzero-value "purchase" (the new CFO's Feb-24-2026 transaction) looks more like an option exercise / onboarding equity conversion than a discretionary market buy. Distinguishing the two: open-market buys with the executive's own cash, signaling incremental conviction, do not appear in this dataset; scheduled vesting and 10b5-1/sell-to-cover administration dominate.

**Market-conduct timing check:** the CEO's Mar-16-2026 sale falls between the FY25 10-K/Q4 print (filed Feb-13-2026) and the Q1 FY26 earnings call (May-06-2026); a scan of the CIQ Key Developments feed for the two weeks following Mar-16-2026 found no Uber-specific adverse announcement in the pool, and the transaction occurred on the same recurring 16th-of-the-month cycle as every other month in the window — timing looks mechanical, not opportunistic [CIQ Key Developments export, entries Mar-16 through Mar-31-2026 scanned; no Uber-specific negative item found].

## 3. Pledging & Encumbrance

| Insider | Shares Pledged | % of Their Holding | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|

**No pledging disclosed — and pledging is contractually prohibited.** Uber's Insider Trading Policy states explicitly: *"Hedging, Pledging and Lending Prohibited. You are prohibited from hedging, pledging and lending Company securities in any transaction, including by entering into any short sales, swaps, options, puts, calls, forward contracts or any other similar derivatives transaction"* [FY25 10-K, Insider Trading Policy §H]. The only "pledge" language elsewhere in the 10-K concerns Uber's own corporate collateral arrangements — its Aurora Innovation Class A common stock pledged as collateral for the company's 2028 Exchangeable Senior Notes, and restricted cash pledged for insurance/letters-of-credit — both corporate financing pledges of investment/cash assets, unrelated to insider equity pledging [FY25 10-K, Note 8 (Long-Term Debt), p.~18289; Note on restricted cash, p.~16312]. This reconciles with `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, which independently confirms "No disclosed insider share pledging found" and scores the pledging disqualifier 0% against a ≥40–50% threshold [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, Disqualifier #2]. No RF-OWN-001/002 red flag fires — there is no pool evidence of any pledge to flag.

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | None. Uber has a single class of common stock, par value $0.00001 per share, one vote per share — no Class A/B or super-voting structure disclosed on the filing's own cover page | [FY25 10-K, cover page, p.221] |
| Controlled-company status | Not a controlled company. 1,207 holders of record as of Feb-10-2026; 99.7% public float; no single holder or bloc exceeds 10% (largest is BlackRock at 7.417%, a passive/index-style manager, not a control investor) | [FY25 10-K, p.5289]; [Public Company Profile — Float % 99.7%]; [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders] |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | Public Investment Fund (Saudi Arabia's sovereign wealth fund) holds 3.578% of CSO ($5,216.1mm) and has one board seat: Turqi Abdulrahman-A. Alnowaiser, PIF's Deputy Governor & Head of International Investment, has served as an Independent Director of Uber since Nov-16-2023. No board-nomination agreement, veto right, or standstill terms tied to this stake are disclosed in the pool — it reads as a large minority financial stake with governance access via one seat, not a controlling or veto bloc | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders (Institutions)]; [Board Members export, Alnowaiser bio] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | **Not applicable — no controlling owner exists.** Ownership is dispersed across index/active institutional managers (BlackRock, Capital Research and Management, Vanguard entities, State Street, Norges Bank Investment Management, Pershing Square) with no bloc above ~7.4%. PIF's 3.6% stake plus one board seat is a sizeable minority financial position, not control (it does not approach a majority, does not carry disclosed veto/nomination rights beyond board representation, and Uber's other governance features — single share class, no controlled-company exemption used — are consistent with dispersed ownership). Filter 6 does not trip; no RF-OWN-004 | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top 25 Holders]; [FY25 10-K, p.5289, p.221] |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6).** Uber is not a government-controlled entity, not a listed subsidiary of a value-maximizing parent, and not a sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate — none of the three structural-conflict patterns applies. The company is widely held with a professionally-managed board and no controlling shareholder. The one governance item worth carrying forward as a monitoring point, not a Filter-6 trip, is the sovereign-wealth-fund board seat (PIF/Alnowaiser): it gives a state-linked investor a voice at the table without giving it control, and its presence should be weighed alongside `05_board-and-shareholder-rights` on independence and related-party screening, not treated as a value-trap signal here.

## 4A. Market Conduct

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Insider buys/sells vs subsequent results (timing) | CEO's Mar-16-2026 $18.9mm sale sits mid-quarter (between the Feb-13-2026 10-K and the May-06-2026 Q1 call) on the same recurring monthly cycle as every other month — no adverse Uber-specific news found in the two weeks following in the Key Developments feed. No pattern of insider selling immediately preceding weak results was found in the pool | [CIQ Report Landscape, Recent Insider Sales]; [CIQ Key Developments export, Mar-16 to Mar-31-2026 entries scanned] |
| Unusual price / volume before announcements | Not established from available data — the ownership/insider-transaction export in this pool does not carry a daily price/volume series matched to announcement dates; a dedicated volume/price-action review ahead of specific 8-K filings is outside this export's scope and was not performed here | Not proven from available data |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | None found in the pool | Not proven from available data |
| Institutional (comparable to FII/DII) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Mixed and one internal-data artifact to flag: (1) the CIQ "Top Sellers" table shows "The Vanguard Group, Inc." reduced to 0 (−192,499,602 sh), while the "Top Buyers" table shows three Vanguard sub-entities (Vanguard Capital Management, Vanguard Portfolio Management, Vanguard Fiduciary Trust Company) newly holding a combined +183,688,874 sh — this reads as an internal CIQ reclassification of the same underlying Vanguard-managed position across sub-entity buckets, not a real economic sale, and should not be read as Vanguard exiting Uber; (2) Toyota Motor Corporation fully exited its direct equity position (−5,125,868 sh to 0, confirmed in both the Top 25 Holders (Corporations) table showing 0 and the Top Sellers table), even though Uber's separate "Current and Pending Investors" relationship list still names Toyota Motor Corporation and Toyota Financial Services Corporation as current investors — a stale/conflicting data point; the more current holdings tables are treated as authoritative per the conservative-default rule; (3) smaller real trims: FMR LLC (−5,343,169 sh, ~21% of its prior position), Cantillon Capital Management (−4,954,899 sh), Marshall Wace (−4,462,774 sh) — none individually large enough (all <0.3% of shares out) to read as a high-conviction high-quality-holder exit | [CIQ Report Landscape, Top Buyers / Top Sellers / Top 25 Holders (Corporations)]; [Public Company Profile, "Current and Pending Investors" list] |

No RF-MKT-001 (insider selling before weak results) or RF-MKT-002 (unusual price/volume before an announcement) fires — the evidence available does not support either trigger; both rows above are stated as "not established" rather than cleared, since the pool does not carry the price/volume dataset that would be needed to test RF-MKT-002 directly.

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Skin in the game is thin: insiders own 0.18% of Uber, and every identifiable "purchase" in the last six months is either a $0-value RSU vesting credit or an ambiguous nonzero-value transaction tied to the new CFO's onboarding — no executive bought Uber stock with their own cash in the window covered by this pool. The net insider signal is mildly negative in dollar terms (led by the CEO's $18.9mm March sale) but immaterial in share-count terms and explained by mechanical vest-and-withhold administration rather than a discretionary bearish call, and no evidence ties any sale to timing ahead of bad news. The one real structural risk to watch for minority holders is not pledging (contractually banned and undisclosed — a clean read) or dual-class control (none exists) but the absence of any controlling or founder shareholder at all combined with a Saudi sovereign-wealth-fund board seat (PIF, 3.6%, one director) — a governance configuration to monitor via board independence and related-party screening (`05`), not a value-trap or control-abuse signal on the evidence here.

## management-governance / 05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md

_Source: `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`_

# Board & Shareholder Rights — UBER

**Partial-data notice.** The 2026 DEF 14A Proxy Statement is not in this data pool. Uber's FY2025 10-K explicitly defers Items 10 (directors/governance detail), 12 (beneficial ownership), 13 (related-person transactions policy), and 14 (accounting fees) to that proxy [FY25 10-K, Items 10/12/13/14]. This report uses the CIQ Board Members export (current roster, tenure, committee membership), the 10-K's own risk-factor disclosure of anti-takeover-adjacent charter/bylaw provisions, the 10-K's related-party notes, and the CIQ Key Developments feed (AGM agenda). It cannot verify individual director attendance, formal committee charters, AGM vote tallies, insider/director ownership percentages, or the written-consent/majority-vs-plurality voting standard — each is marked Insufficient Data below rather than assumed.

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 10 voting directors (Sugar, Khosrowshahi, Eckert, Thain, Advaithi, Alnowaiser, Arora, Burns, Ginsberg, Wynaendts). One additional non-voting Board Observer (co-founder Garrett Camp, observer since May 2020, no board vote) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export); Key Developments, AGM agenda "to elect the ten director nominees" |
| Independent directors (%) | 9 of 10 voting directors are labeled Independent Director (90%); the sole non-independent voting director is CEO Dara Khosrowshahi | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |
| Chair / CEO split? | Yes — Independent Chairman Ronald D. Sugar (since Aug-2018) is separate from CEO Dara Khosrowshahi (director since Sep-2017, not Chair) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |
| Audit Committee independence | 5/5 independent (Chair: John A. Thain; members: Advaithi, Alnowaiser, Burns, Wynaendts). Thain's background (former President/COO, The Goldman Sachs Group; former CEO, NYSE Group/NYSE Euronext; former Chairman/CEO, CIT Group) suggests real financial fluency, though a formal SEC "audit committee financial expert" designation is not confirmed from this pool (that label lives in the proxy) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |
| Compensation Committee independence | 4/4 independent (Chair: Robert A. Eckert; members: Sugar, Arora, Ginsberg) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |
| Nominating Committee independence | 4/4 independent (Chair: Sugar; members: Eckert, Arora, Ginsberg) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |
| Corporate Governance Committee independence | 5/5 independent (Chair: Sugar; members: Eckert, Arora, Ginsberg, Burns) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |

Every standing committee this export names is composed entirely of independent directors — the CEO sits on none of the four. That clears the legal/box-ticking bar for independence cleanly. Whether it is also *effective* independence is a separate question addressed in §2 and §5.

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Individual director tenure (years, as of Aug-2026) | Sugar ~8.0 (since Aug-2018); Khosrowshahi ~9.0 (CEO, since Sep-2017); Eckert ~6.4 (since Mar-2020); Thain ~9.0 (since 2017); Advaithi ~6.1 (since Jul-2020); Alnowaiser ~2.7 (since Nov-2023); Arora ~1.2 (since Jun-2025); Burns ~8.8 (since Oct-2017); Ginsberg ~6.5 (since Feb-2020); Wynaendts ~5.4 (since Mar-2021) | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export), "Years on Board" field per director |
| Average director tenure | ~6.3 years across all 10 voting directors; ~6.0 years across the 9 independent directors | Calculated from the row above |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | Not stale — no single director exceeds ~9 years, and three directors joined within the last 5 years (Wynaendts 2021, Alnowaiser 2023, Arora 2025). **Overboarding is a real, named concern for two directors:** (1) Turqi Alnowaiser holds a full-time executive role as Deputy Governor & Head of International Investment at Saudi Arabia's Public Investment Fund (PIF) *and* is simultaneously Independent Chairman of Lucid Group (NasdaqGS:LCID), an Independent Member of Deutsche-Bank-adjacent Hapag-Lloyd's Supervisory Board (XTRA:HLAG), and holds three further Heathrow-airport-group directorships (Heathrow Airport Holdings, FGP Topco, LHR Airports) alongside Uber — a demanding combination of one full-time C-suite role plus at least three other board seats; (2) Nikesh Arora is the sitting Chairman & CEO of Palo Alto Networks (a large, complex public company) while also serving on Compagnie Financière Richemont SA's board and Uber's board — two outside public-company boards on top of a full-time CEO role | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export), individual "Other Boards" / "Job Functions" fields |
| Recent refreshment | Two of nine independent directors added in the last ~3 years (Alnowaiser Nov-2023, Arora Jun-2025) — genuine, not cosmetic, refreshment; the longest-serving independent directors (Thain, Burns, both ~9 years) pre-date the 2019 IPO | Board Members.rtf (CIQ export) |

**A related-party-adjacent independence nuance, not currently in the RPT table below because it is not a transaction:** Uber's own CIQ investor list names "Public Investment Fund (Yasir Bin Othman H. Al-Rumayyan)" as a current investor in the company [Public Company Profile, "Current and Pending Investors"]. PIF was one of Uber's largest pre-IPO investors (a widely reported ~$3.5bn investment in 2016); its current % stake is not disclosed in this pool (deferred to the absent proxy, Item 12). Director Turqi Alnowaiser's day job is PIF's Deputy Governor & Head of International Investment. He is labeled "Independent Director" under NYSE's listing-standard test (no direct employment or compensation tie to Uber itself), but in substance he is the sitting Uber board's most direct link to one of its largest historical outside shareholders. That is not evidence of misconduct, and NYSE's technical independence test may well be satisfied — but it is a real difference between *legal* independence and a *fully arm's-length* voice on matters where PIF's own interests as a shareholder could diverge from other minority holders' (e.g., control transactions, capital return policy). Flagged as a monitoring item, not a red flag on the evidence available.

## 3. Related-Party Transactions & Group Leakage

| RPT Type | Amount | % of Revenue | % of PAT | % of Net Worth | Arm's Length? | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| Term loan receivable — Moove Cars Mobility, S.L. ("Moove," an EV-fleet financing partner in which Uber also holds preferred equity) | $384mm (Dec-31-2025), up from $288mm (Dec-31-2024), +33% YoY | 0.74% | 3.82% | 1.38% | Structured as an arm's-length commercial loan under a 2021 series of agreements (equity investment + term loan + commercial partnership); no proxy-level RPT-approval narrative available to independently confirm audit-committee sign-off | FY25 10-K, Note 15 (Loans and Guarantees / Related Party) |
| Convertible note receivable — Neutron Holdings, Inc. ("Lime") | $119mm (Dec-31-2025), down from $144mm (Dec-31-2024), −17% YoY | 0.23% | 1.18% | 0.43% | Related-party status arises solely from Uber's equity stake in Lime common stock, not from operating purchases/sales | FY25 10-K, Note 6 (Investments) |
| Equity-method investment — Careem Technologies (Careem's non-ridesharing business, majority sold to e& in Dec-2023) | Initial fair value $300mm (Dec-2023); Uber retains ~42% equity interest and two board seats; basis difference "not material" at Dec-2025 | n/m (investment, not P&L) | n/m | 1.07% (vs. initial value) | Arm's-length divestiture to a third party (Emirates Telecommunications Group, "e&"); Uber realized a $204mm gain on the transaction | FY25 10-K, Note 4/5 (Equity Method Investments) |
| Sales to / purchases from related parties (recurring, P&L) | None disclosed | — | — | — | N/A | FY25 10-K; confirmed by `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, RPT test |
| Rent / royalty / management fees to a promoter or controlling group | Not applicable — no promoter or controlling shareholder exists | — | — | — | N/A | 00_governance-data-triage.md, §Control structure |

FY2025 basis figures used above: Revenue $52,017mm; net income attributable to Uber Technologies, Inc. (PAT) $10,053mm; total equity (net worth) $27,918mm [FY25 10-K, Consolidated Statements of Operations / Balance Sheets].

**Read.** Apply the module's RPT materiality thresholds (Low <1% / Medium 1–5% / High 5–10% / Critical >10% of revenue): every individual related-party balance sits in the Low-to-Medium band on a revenue basis, and the combined related-party balance-sheet exposure (~$803mm) is roughly 1.5% of revenue and 2.9% of net worth — nowhere near the 25%-of-revenue/expenses hard disqualifier owned by `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (which found 0.7% on the largest single item and confirmed no disqualifier triggered). These are **not promoter self-dealing** in the classic sense: Uber has no promoter or controlling shareholder, and each related party (Moove, Lime, Careem Technologies) became "related" only because Uber holds an equity or lending position in a commercial partner or a business it once controlled — the opposite direction of leakage from a typical promoter-siphoning RPT. The one genuine watch item is growth: the Moove loan grew 33% YoY against 18% revenue growth, and no RPT-approval-policy narrative (which would normally confirm audit-committee or minority sign-off) is available in this pool — that detail lives in the absent 2026 proxy (Item 13). **No material related-party leakage is evidenced; the RPT-approval process itself is unconfirmed.**

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill (active shareholder rights plan) | Not confirmed active, but enabled | No currently-adopted rights plan is disclosed in this pool. The certificate of incorporation authorizes the board to issue "undesignated preferred stock" without stockholder approval — a standard "blank-check preferred" provision that functions as a poison-pill enabler even without an active plan today | FY25 10-K, Risk Factors, "provisions...could depress the trading price...by acting to discourage, delay, or prevent a change of control" |
| Classified / staggered board | Not present (declassified — annual election of the full board) | The 10-K's own list of anti-takeover provisions does not include a staggered board, and the May-2026 AGM agenda calls for electing "the ten director nominees" — i.e., the entire board stands for election every year | FY25 10-K, Risk Factors; Key Developments.rtf, AGM notice, May-04-2026 |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | Not present | Single class of common stock, par value $0.00001; no separate super-voting class disclosed on the filing cover page or in the risk factors | FY25 10-K cover page; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Not disclosed in this pool | Not addressed in the 10-K text; this standard is set in the bylaws/proxy, neither of which is in the pool | Not assessable — proxy/bylaws exhibit needed |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Restricted (special meetings); written-consent standard not disclosed | The 10-K explicitly flags, as a risk factor to investors, "limitations on stockholder ability to convene special stockholder meetings"; it also flags "prohibition on cumulative voting in the election of directors, which limits the ability of minority stockholders to elect director candidates," advance-notice requirements for stockholder proposals, and that the board (not stockholders) fills board vacancies. Whether stockholders retain a right to act by written consent is not addressed in the extracted text | FY25 10-K, Risk Factors ("Delaware law and provisions in our...certificate of incorporation and...bylaws could make a merger, tender offer, or proxy contest difficult...") |

**Read.** Uber's charter/bylaw basket is a genuine, disclosed set of entrenchment tools — no cumulative voting, limited shareholder right to call special meetings, board-controlled vacancy-filling, advance-notice bylaws, and blank-check preferred authority — all listed by the company itself as provisions that "could depress the trading price...by acting to discourage, delay, or prevent a change of control." These are common among US tech IPOs and are not unusual by market standards, but they are real, not theoretical. They are meaningfully offset by three shareholder-favorable structural facts: single-class stock (one vote per share, no founder super-voting block), an annually-elected (non-staggered) board, and no controlling shareholder to entrench in the first place — the tools exist, but there is no controlling holder positioned to use them against the rest of the shareholder base the way a promoter-controlled company would.

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| AGM votes against (remuneration / RPT / director / auditor resolutions) | Not disclosed in this pool. The May-04-2026 AGM agenda is known (elect 10 directors; non-binding Say-on-Pay; non-binding Say-on-Pay-frequency vote; ratify PwC as auditor for 2026) but the vote-result tally (an 8-K filing) is not in the pool | Key Developments.rtf, AGM notice, May-04-2026 |
| Proxy-advisor recommendations (ISS / Glass Lewis / IiAS) | Not disclosed in this pool | Not assessable |
| Institutional opposition to any resolution | Not disclosed in this pool | Not assessable |
| Dilution history (preferential allotment, warrants, QIP, ESOP pool) | Shares outstanding rose every year from IPO through Dec-2024 (2,089.0mm, driven by heavy stock-based compensation — $1,935mm FY2023, $1,796mm FY2024, $1,826mm FY2025 company-wide SBC expense), then reversed: 2,058.1mm at Dec-31-2025, 2,035.6mm at Mar-31-2026, as a $27.0bn buyback authorization ($7.0bn Feb-2024 + $20.0bn added Jul-2025; $19.2bn undrawn at Dec-31-2025) began outrunning SBC-driven dilution for the first time since the 2019 IPO | `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, Net share count trajectory row; FY25 10-K, Note 10 |

No AGM vote-result data of any kind is available in this pool, so a >20%-votes-against test (this module's RF-SHR-001 trigger) cannot be run in either direction — this is marked Insufficient Data, not a clean pass. Dilution has reversed direction and is not, on the evidence available, an active minority-shareholder concern; the multi-year SBC-driven dilution before FY2025 was disclosed plainly in the 10-K, not buried.

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

On paper, Uber's board clears almost every formal governance box: 90% independent directors, a fully independent chair separate from the CEO, four committees that are 100% independent-director-composed, single-class one-share-one-vote stock, no controlling shareholder or promoter to entrench, and an annually-elected (non-staggered) board that has genuinely refreshed (2 of 9 independent directors added in the last 3 years). Related-party exposure is small (≤1.4% of net worth on any single item) and structurally different from classic promoter self-dealing — it exists because Uber holds equity or lending positions in commercial partners (Moove, Lime, Careem Technologies), not because a controller is extracting value from the company.

Set against that: the charter/bylaws contain a real, company-disclosed basket of entrenchment tools (no cumulative voting, restricted ability to call special meetings, board-controlled vacancy-filling, blank-check preferred authority), a director with a full-time executive role at one of Uber's largest historical shareholders (PIF) sits on the board carrying the "independent" label, two directors (Alnowaiser, Arora) carry board loads that raise a genuine capacity question, and — most materially — an unresolved shareholder derivative lawsuit filed in federal court (N.D. Cal., reported Jun/Jul-2026) names the sitting CEO, the independent Chairman, and every sitting director, alleging the board's own 2024–2026 proxy statements about safety oversight were false and that a safety-risk model was ignored for five years (2017–2022) [carried from `01_management-and-track-record.md`, RF-MGT-005, confidence 2/5, unadjudicated]. That allegation is unproven, but it is precisely the question this section exists to ask: is this independent-on-paper board actually functioning as a check on management, or box-ticking? Until the litigation resolves, that question cannot be answered in the board's favor. The absence of the 2026 proxy also means insider/director ownership percentages, AGM vote margins, and any RPT-approval process cannot be independently verified from this pool.

**Verdict: adequate.** The structural bones (independence, split chair, single-class stock, no promoter) are genuinely good and support real shareholder protection in the ordinary course. The verdict is not "strong protection" because of the disclosed anti-takeover basket, the PIF-affiliated "independent" director, the overboarding on two seats, and — above all — the unresolved allegation that this same board's own oversight disclosures were false. It is not "weak / entrenched" because there is no controlling shareholder, no dual-class structure, and no proven abuse on the evidence in this pool. The single biggest entrenchment/self-dealing risk to minority holders is not related-party leakage (which is small and non-promoter) — it is the unresolved question of whether the board's stated safety-oversight function was real, which is now the subject of active federal litigation naming every sitting director.

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — UBER

**Data available:** two verbatim earnings-call transcripts (Q1 FY2026, May-06-2026; Q2 FY2026, Aug-05-2026) — no paraphrase or press-release substitute used. No shareholder letters exist for this filer (US SEC regime; the module rule requires transcripts OR letters, and transcripts are present, so no partial-data cap applies on that ground). `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` is available and used as the primary non-GAAP cross-check. Both available transcripts cover quarters management itself characterized as strong ("outstanding," "exceptional start") — there is no genuinely bad quarter in the pool to test tone against; the closest available "bad news" data points are a narrow revenue miss vs. consensus, a take-rate compression, and the quiet discontinuation of a headline metric, all assessed below. This is flagged as a real limitation on the "tone in bad times" read (Section 4), not papered over.

## 1. Owning the Misses

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Revenue miss vs. consensus, Q1 FY26 (−0.45%) and Q2 FY26 (−0.52%) | Not raised proactively in either CEO's prepared remarks (which led with Gross Bookings, non-GAAP EPS growth, and buyback size). When an analyst (Justin Post, BofA) asked about take rates on the Q2 call, CFO Balaji Krishnamurthy gave a specific, quantified answer: mobility take rate down ~500bps YoY, "about 400 basis points is entirely related to this U.K. business model change, and it's an optical impact" | **Partially owned** — specific and quantified once asked, but led with the positive framing and did not volunteer the take-rate/revenue story up front | Q2 FY26 transcript, Q&A (Justin Post exchange); `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` §6 |
| FY2025 revenue-growth deceleration cited by `earnings/08` (18.3%→14.5%→12.2% YoY over three quarters) | Never named as a deceleration trend by management in either transcript's prepared remarks or Q&A; framed instead through Gross Bookings growth (which held above 20%), a different metric that does not decelerate the same way | **Obscured by omission** — the number investors would extrapolate (revenue) was not addressed on its own terms; the number management chose to lead with (Gross Bookings) was | `earnings/08_earnings-red-flags.md` §2.2–2.3; Q1/Q2 FY26 transcripts (full-text absence of "deceleration"/revenue-growth-rate discussion) |
| Discontinuation of the company-level Adjusted-EBITDA-to-GAAP reconciliation, effective Q1 FY2026, while guidance-beat claims continued | Zero mentions of "Adjusted EBITDA" in either the Q1 or Q2 FY2026 transcript (full-text search) — no acknowledgment that the metric was dropped, no explanation offered, no analyst asked about it either | **Obscured** — a material disclosure reduction executed silently, not disclosed as a change on the call | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §4; `01_management-and-track-record.md` Finding 01-010 (RF-DISC-001) |
| Two federal-jury MDL verdicts (Arizona, $8.5mm, Feb-2026; Charlotte NC, $5,000, Apr-2026) | Not named in the three most recent filings in the pool (FY25 10-K, Q1 FY26 10-Q, Q2 FY26 10-Q) even though an aggregate litigation reserve is disclosed | **Obscured** — specific, adjudicated, publicly known verdicts omitted from the filings that should carry them | `red_flags.csv`, RF-REG-002; FY25 10-K; Q1/Q2 FY26 10-Q |
| CFO transition, Feb-16-2026 ("This termination is not the result of any disagreements concerning financial disclosures or accounting matters") | Direct, specific, affirmatively-worded disclaimer issued at the time of the event, not vague "personal reasons" boilerplate | **Owned** — plainly stated, though a company self-representation that this agent cannot independently verify | CIQ Key Developments, 2026-02-16; `01_management-and-track-record.md` §4 |
| Adjusted EBITDA 3-yr growth target (Feb-2024 Investor Day: high-30s–40% CAGR) vs. FY2024→FY2025 actual growth of 34.6%, just below the low end | Never referenced against the original 2024 target in either FY2026 transcript — no reconciliation to the Investor Day framework was offered or requested | **Not addressed** — a near-miss on the company's own headline target was left unmentioned rather than owned or explained | `01_management-and-track-record.md` Finding 01-006; Q1/Q2 FY26 transcripts (no mention of "Investor Day" or "three-year" target found) |

**Read:** The pattern is consistent — when an analyst asks a pointed, quantitative question, management answers with real numbers (the UK take-rate breakdown is genuinely specific, not deflected). But nothing negative is volunteered first, and the two largest disclosure-quality issues in this record — dropping Adjusted EBITDA and omitting two named litigation verdicts — were not surfaced by management at all; they were found by cross-referencing filings against a vendor litigation feed and a full-text search of the calls, not by anything management said.

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | "Legal, non-income tax, and regulatory reserve changes and settlements" was added back to Adjusted EBITDA as if non-recurring in both FY2024 ($1,123M) and FY2025 ($564M) — a charge that recurs every year the company discloses is not a one-off by definition. `RF-DISC-002` triggered | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §4–5; FY25 10-K, Item 7 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | FY2025: unadjusted EBITDA $6,312M → company Adjusted EBITDA $8,730M, a +38.3% gap ($2,418M of add-backs). FY2024: unadjusted EBITDA $3,536M → Adjusted EBITDA $6,484M, a +83.4% gap. The gap is narrowing in percentage terms as core operations improve, but total non-D&A add-backs were still 43.9% of FY2025 Income from Operations ($2,446M ÷ $5,565M) — well above the 15% materiality threshold | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §4, §7; computed from FY25 10-K, Item 7 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Yes, on two fronts: (1) the recurring legal-reserve add-back inflates Adjusted EBITDA every year by treating a repeat charge as non-recurring; (2) GAAP net income/EPS in both FY2024 and FY2025 were separately inflated by one-time, non-cash deferred-tax-asset valuation-allowance releases ($6.4B and $5.0B respectively) that a reader extrapolating GAAP EPS growth would not know to strip out | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5, §9–10 |

`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` scored overall earnings quality 71/100 ("mostly clean but some adjustment noise") — the underlying cash generation is genuinely strong (CFO exceeded unadjusted EBITDA 160–202% of EBITDA in FY2023–2025), but the two headline profitability numbers most investors default to (GAAP EPS, Adjusted EBITDA) both carry disclosed, real distortions. This module's read is narrower and harsher than that overall 71: candor is not about whether the cash is real (it is), it is about whether the *labels* on the adjustments are honest, and the "non-recurring" legal-reserve line recurring two years straight fails that specific test.

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Segment-level KPIs (Gross Bookings, Trips, Monthly Active Platform Consumers, Mobility/Delivery/Freight growth rates) remain disclosed consistently, and the segment profit metric was formally renamed from "Segment Adjusted EBITDA" to "Segment Operating Income" with prior periods recast for comparability — a metric change, but not a silent one. Disclosure got **less transparent** in one specific and material way: the company-level "Adjusted EBITDA" reconciliation that investors used through FY2025 to track margin progress was dropped starting the Q1 FY2026 10-Q, with zero acknowledgment on either subsequent earnings call (full-text search, both transcripts) — a genuine reduction in like-for-like visibility precisely as the two most recent quarters showed take-rate compression [`earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §4]. Separately, two adjudicated MDL jury verdicts were left unnamed in the three most recent filings even though an aggregate litigation reserve is disclosed, which is a timeliness gap on a specific, checkable fact rather than a general "opacity" complaint (`RF-REG-002`).

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

Both transcripts in the pool are from quarters management itself framed as strong — "another outstanding quarter," "an exceptional start to 2026" — so this module cannot test tone against a genuinely weak quarter; that is a real gap, not glossed over. Within what is available, the tone stayed promotional even where the numbers were mixed: the CEO opened Q2 FY26 with "another outstanding quarter... record audience and engagement" before any mention of the revenue miss, the take-rate compression, or the Brazil competitive pressure — all of which surfaced only in Q&A, prompted by analysts, not volunteered. Where analysts did ask pointed quantitative questions (UK take-rate math, Brazil 2-wheeler competition, AI-driven headcount moderation), the CFO and CEO answered with specific numbers rather than deflecting — there is no visible evasiveness on the questions that were actually asked. The gap is not in how management answers hard questions; it is in what gets raised unprompted and what gets left for a sell-side analyst to dig out.

## 5. Candor Read

Uber's management answers specific questions specifically — the UK take-rate breakdown, the Brazil competitive dynamics, and the AI/headcount tradeoff were all quantified clearly under direct questioning, and the guidance-beat record (four straight quarters, above the high end the last two) is genuine and checkable. But the record this module can actually see is thin on genuinely bad news, and on the two disclosure decisions that mattered most — dropping the company-level Adjusted EBITDA reconciliation and leaving two named litigation verdicts out of the filings — nothing was volunteered, nothing was explained, and no analyst caught it either; both were found only by cross-referencing filings and a full-text transcript search. Layered on top is the unresolved shareholder derivative lawsuit (routed from `01_management-and-track-record.md`, `RF-MGT-005`) alleging the sitting CEO and full board made false statements in the 2024–2026 proxies about board safety oversight — an unproven but serious integrity signal naming the exact people this report is judging, which this module lets weigh against the trust read rather than setting aside, per CLAUDE.md §24 Filter 1. The combination of promotional-first framing, a quietly dropped headline metric, and an unresolved integrity allegation is enough to keep this out of "candid/high-trust," while the genuine specificity under direct questioning and the clean cash-generation record (per `earnings/06`) keep it out of "promotional/low-trust." **Verdict: mixed.**

RF-DISC-001 (commentary contradicting the numbers)
RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)
RF-REG-002 (delayed results / material-disclosure timeliness)
